<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a Python function that configures logging based on a JSON configuration file. The configuration file must specify the log level, log file path, and log format. Your function should read the configuration file, validate its contents, and set up logging accordingly. If the configuration file is missing or does not contain the required keys, your function should raise an appropriate exception.

**Input:**
- A JSON configuration file named `logging_config.json` with the following structure:
  ```json
  {
      "log_level": "INFO",
      "log_file_path": "app.log",
      "log_format": "%(asctime)s - %(levelname)s - %(message)s"
  }
  ```

**Output:**
- The function should set up logging according to the configuration file. If the configuration file is missing or invalid, it should raise a `FileNotFoundError` or `ValueError` respectively.

**Sample Input:**
- `logging_config.json`:
  ```json
  {
      "log_level": "DEBUG",
      "log_file_path": "debug.log",
      "log_format": "%(asctime)s - %(levelname)s - %(message)s"
  }
  ```

**Sample Output:**
- The log file `debug.log` should contain log messages with the specified format and level.

```python
import json
import logging
import os

def setup_logging_from_config(config_path='logging_config.json'):
    """
    Set up logging configuration from a JSON configuration file.

    :param config_path: Path to the JSON configuration file.
    :raises FileNotFoundError: If the configuration file does not exist.
    :raises ValueError: If the configuration file does not contain the required keys.
    """
    if not os.path.isfile(config_path):
        raise FileNotFoundError(f"Configuration file '{config_path}' not found.")
    
    with open(config_path, 'r') as config_file:
        config = json.load(config_file)

    expected_keys = {'log_level', 'log_file_path', 'log_format'}
    config_keys = set(config.keys())
    if expected_keys != config_keys:
        raise ValueError(f"Configuration file must contain the following keys: {expected_keys}. Found: {config_keys}")

    logging.basicConfig(
        filename=config['log_file_path'],
        level=config['log_level'].upper(),
        format=config['log_format'],
        filemode='a'
    )
    logging.info(f"Logging setup successfully with config: {config}")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    setup_logging_from_config('logging_config.json')
    logging.debug("This is a debug message")
    logging.info("This is an info message")
    logging.warning("This is a warning message")
    logging.error("This is an error message")
    logging.critical("This is a critical message")
```<#sep#>,35,37.

</think>To find the sum of the numbers in the tenth row of the pattern created by Camila, we need to determine the starting number of the tenth row and the total number of terms in that row. Then, we will use the formula for the sum of an arithmetic series to find the desired sum.

1. **Determine the number of terms in the tenth row:**
   Each row \( n \) contains \( 2n \) numbers. For the tenth row (\( n = 10 \)):
   \[
   \text{Number of terms} = 2 \times 10 = 20
   \]

2. **Find the starting number of the tenth row:**
   The starting number \( S(n) \) of the \( n \)-th row follows the formula:
   \[
   S(n) = 2n^2 - 3n + 3
   \]
   For \( n = 10 \):
   \[
   S(10) = 2(10)^2 - 3(10) + 3 = 200 - 30 + 3 = 173
   \]

3. **Determine the last term of the tenth row:**
   The numbers in each row increase by 2. The last term \( L(n) \) of the \( n \)-th row can be calculated as:
   \[
   L(n) = S(n) + 2(2n - 1)
   \]
   For \( n = 10 \):
   \[
   L(10) = 173 + 2(2 \times 10 - 1) = 173 + 2 \times 19 = 173 + 38 = 211
   \]

4. **Calculate the sum of the tenth row:**
   The sum of an arithmetic series is given by:
   \[
   \text{Sum} = \frac{\text{number of terms}}{2} \times (\text{first term} + \text{last term})
   \]
   For the tenth row:
   \[
   \text{Sum}(10) = \frac{20}{2} \times (173 + 211) = 10 \times 384 = 3840
   \]

Thus, the sum of the numbers in the tenth row is:

<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a function `fetch_weather_data` that interacts with the OpenWeatherMap API to retrieve weather information for a specified city. Your function should accept the city name and an API key as parameters and return a dictionary containing the temperature (in Celsius), humidity (as a percentage), and a brief weather description. If the city is not found or an error occurs, the function should return `None`.

**Sample Input:**
- `city_name = "London"`
- `api_key = "your_api_key_here"`

**Sample Output:**
```python
{
    'temperature': 15.2,
    'humidity': 81,
    'weather_description': 'light rain'
}
```

```python
import requests

def fetch_weather_data(city_name, api_key):
    """
    Fetches weather data for a given city using the OpenWeatherMap API.

    Parameters:
    city_name (str): The name of the city for which to fetch weather data.
    api_key (str): The API key for accessing the OpenWeatherMap API.

    Returns:
    dict or None: A dictionary containing the temperature, humidity, and weather description if successful,
                  or None if the city is not found or an error occurs.
    """
    base_url = "http://api.openweathermap.org/data/2.5/weather?"
    complete_url = f"{base_url}appid={api_key}&q={city_name}&units=metric"
    response = requests.get(complete_url)
    weather_data = response.json()

    if weather_data['cod'] == 200:
        temperature = weather_data['main']['temp']
        humidity = weather_data['main']['humidity']
        weather_description = weather_data['weather'][0]['description']
        return {
            'temperature': temperature,
            'humidity': humidity,
            'weather_description': weather_description
        }
    else:
        return None

<#sep#>You are given a list of strings. Your task is to process each string by removing all duplicate characters, retaining only the first occurrence of each character. The order of characters in the output strings should match their first appearance in the input strings.

**Input:**
- A list of strings, where each string consists of lowercase English letters.
- The length of the list is at most 100.
- Each string has a length of at most 100.

**Output:**
- A list of strings with duplicates removed as described.

**Sample Input:**
```
["hello", "world"]
```

**Sample Output:**
```
["helo", "wrd"]
```

```python
def remove_duplicates(strings):
    """
    Removes duplicate characters from each string in the list, keeping only the first occurrence of each character.

    :param strings: List of strings to process.
    :return: List of strings with duplicates removed.
    """
    result = []
    for string in strings:
        unique_chars = []
        for char in string:
            if char not in unique_chars:
                unique_chars.append(char)
        result.append(''.join(unique_chars))
    return result

# Example usage:
input_list = ["hello", "world"]
output_list = remove_duplicates(input_list)
print(output_list)  # Output: ['helo', 'wrd']
